ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u2	We continue with the debate in section 22, Ministry of Territorial Policy. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Territorial Policy.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u3	Good morning. Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear in this House to present to you the budget estimates of the Ministry of Territorial Policy for the year 2022. But let me say that before we begin to conclude the agreements that have already been announced and that make it possible for the parliamentary processing of these General State Budgets, which will bring progress and prosperity to our country. [[Applauds]] I would like to thank the political groups, all the political formations that will support them, because they also support the development of Spain. Once again it is shown that dialogue is the way forward, especially when it comes to Parliament. This Government is fulfilling, and the budgets will come into force in time and form for the second time in this legislature, fostering stability, confidence and security for our country. I will focus my speech today on the broad lines of section 22, but first I would like to make some general considerations. The Government is promoting a transformative recovery based on solidarity between individuals, territories and generations. A reactivation in which the protagonist is the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, which also has its reflection in section 22. The General State Budgets for 2022 are public accounts anchored in social justice, in the defence of public services, in the commitment to democratic quality and in the strengthening of cohesion. The commitment to territorial cohesion, in particular, translates into increased investment in infrastructure, as well as direct contributions to the autonomous communities and local entities, which reached record figures in these accounts. Let me give you some information: in these budgets, more than EUR 10 billion of the Recovery and Resilience Facility is made available to the territorial administrations. The autonomous communities will count next year with a total of 126,500 million euros - cipherinedite - between direct financing, compensations and European funds. The municipalities will receive, for their part, the largest deliveries on account of their entire history. They will grow by 13% to 22,062 million. These are budgets that are also firmly committed to the demographic challenge and depopulation of some areas of Spain. These policies will have an allocation of more than €42 billion. They highlight games for digital connectivity, tourism, agricultural and livestock sustainability and also to improve the accessibility of public services. These budgets also increase the Ministry's budget, reaching almost EUR 510 million,76 million more than in 2021, i.e. a 17% increase. However, ladies and gentlemen, territorial policy, as you know, goes far beyond economic investment. To make territorial policy requires the articulation of mechanisms for dialogue and cooperation between the different territorial levels. The budget for 2022 consolidates these three lines of action for the ministry: strengthening inter-territorial collaboration, strengthening local entities and improving the provision of the services of the General Administration of the State in the territory. Starting with interterritorial collaboration, the 922.M program promotes and develops collaboration between the three levels of government: the State, the autonomous communities and the local entities. It will be provided with 5% more than the previous year, EUR 10.8 million. I would like to stress that in the framework of multilateral cooperation we have made a proposal to improve the functioning of the Conference of Presidents, which is called upon to be a key vault in our system of co-governance. We are going to propose the creation of a permanent secretariat to promote its coordination and to continue its work. This very week, next Friday, I will meet with the preparatory committee for the impetus and follow-up to the Conference of Presidents with all the autonomous communities, in order to make progress on certain matters, such as the reform of this regulation. Secondly, we will continue to devote all our efforts to dialogue with a view to reaching agreements with all administrations. We will continue to promote regulatory collaboration within the framework of article 33.2 of the Organic Law of the Constitutional Court. The objective we have set ourselves is to reduce this competitive conflict, and already since June 2018 this dialogue has allowed it to be reduced to very important limits, up to 74%. Only seventy-four agreements have been reached in this legislature within the framework of the bilateral cooperation commissions, a figure that shows how much progress can be made when dialogue and institutional loyalty prevail, how conflicts languish in the face of politics. Following the strengthening of inter-territorial collaboration, the 92.KC framework programme will invest EUR 2 million this year, mainly in a technological platform for inter-administrative collaboration that supports all systems of intergovernmental relations, and I refer to sectoral conferences, bilateral commissions, inter-administrative agreements, etc. It is a platform that will facilitate the exchange of information and networking in the different cooperation bodies. Under the same heading we are also going to undertake the development of the application that already exists for the management of aid to local authorities in the face of emergency situations of civil protection. We will automate data collection and improve information systems to further reduce processing times in this type of aid. Beyond processing, we also bet to promote collaboration between administrations in emergency situations. This will be another matter we want to address in the context of the Conference of Presidents. Not in vain, the collaboration has allowed us to respond in an exemplary way to the health crisis and also to give a joint response to other more recent emergencies such as that of the volcano of La Palma, and I take the opportunity to send our solidarity, support and permanent commitment to palm trees and palm trees. [[Applauds]] The second line of action is the strengthening of local entities or what is the same, ladies and gentlemen: a bet decided by municipalism. To this end, the 94.KC programme will allocate EUR 149 million for the modernisation and digitisation of local administrations. As you know, this amount is part of a global investment amounting to 192 million euros for the period 2021-2023. From the ministry we have already launched the part of the investment that would correspond to this current year, 2021, with a first tranche of 92 million euros destined for the municipalities with more than 50 000 inhabitants. This investment will allow financing projects of actions as important for municipalities as improving their municipal services through digital platforms that pay attention to citizens and businesses with the development of mobile or other applications. Last week they presented in Badalona an important application that will facilitate the lives of the people and that had to do with the measurement of the traffic lights both on the beach and in the parking lots of the promenade to make the decision to go or not, depending on how these traffic lights were. These investments will enable citizens, businesses and public employees to democratize access to data. These actions will also be aimed at maintaining the cybersecurity of local authorities or the active use of emerging technologies, without a doubt exerting a decisive impetus for the modernisation of local administrations. With these projects we will get digitization to all territories, to any small municipality. In the same vein, in order not to leave anyone behind, budget programme 942.A envisages an increase in contributions to local cooperation that will exceed 36 million euros. Specifically, under this heading we will finance the transfer of the Investment Fund to Teruel - which, as you know, has a long tradition - and we will also increase the subsidy to the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces by up to 20% compared to the previous budgets. In fact, this Government has intensified cooperation with the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces, giving prominence to the municipalities, their mayors and mayors, taking on the commitment to promote instruments to improve institutionalization and their participation in the various forums. With regard to the strengthening of local authorities, let me also remind you that from the ministry we are working on amending the Local Regulatory Law, whose preliminary draft we will bring to the Council of Ministers before the end of this year. This is a measure that does not require a great amount of budgetary investment but shows the priority for the Government and the priority for the ministry. We are talking about a legal change that makes it possible to clarify the competition system and increase the room for manoeuvre of local authorities in the provision of services, because this Government, as I said to you, ladies and gentlemen, has full confidence in the municipalities and a deep respect for their local autonomy. The reform of the Law Regulating the Basis of Local Regime will also include specific regulations for municipalities with smaller populations and at risk of depopulation, with the aim of facilitating public management and contributing to the fixing of population throughout the territory by providing quality basic services. The third line of action that I wanted to mark in this intervention concerns the improvement of the provision of the quality of the services of the General Administration of the State in the territory, which is carried out through a strategic plan for the improvement of these services. Specifically, budget programme 921.P, which allocates EUR 272 million to the activities of delegations and sub-delegations of Government, means an increase of more than EUR 6 million over the previous year; of these EUR 3.5 million will be devoted to investment in modernization and technological renewal also in this area, with the aim of providing better services to citizens in foreign offices, in international vaccination centres, in border inspection posts or in coordination units against violence against women. And let me also take this opportunity to acknowledge the work of public employees working in these gender-based violence coordination units on the eve of the International Day against Gender-based Violence. [[Applauds]] Increasing this investment for us is crucial, because strengthening the General Administration of the State in the territory is also a way of making state. Ladies and gentlemen, I do not want to conclude my speech without sharing with you a reflection that is also a conviction, we have emerged from this crisis with really drastic consequences for all Spanish citizens, especially those who lost loved ones, but we have learned some very valuable lessons that should never fall into a broken bag. One of them is that joint work makes each and every one of us and at all levels of government stronger. Yesterday I listened with pride to the President of the European Commission to highlight the effort that we have made in this country by all administrations to achieve a record figure of 90% vaccination, and I said that this collective success of 90% vaccination made the risk of dying thirty times lower than that of other countries in our environment. No doubt, I think it's a success to celebrate. We're saving lives. Collaboration allows us to respond to unique needs in each territory without hindering shared goals and challenges. In this regard, I firmly believe that this draft budget that we are discussing today will bring growth and well-being throughout the territory, to the whole of the citizenry, because it dismantles barriers, because it fights against discrimination and social exclusion, because it promotes gender equality, because it takes into account the different needs of all the territories and their diversity, because it addresses the existing gaps, not the obvious ones, and also because it advances the predictable ones, such as the generational or the digital ones. In short, they are budgets that will allow us to move forward and grow as a country. Ladies and gentlemen, I finish as I began by thanking all the political groups that have decided to support these budgets and therefore support the progress and recovery of Spain, and also all those political forces that have made an effort to study them, to table amendments, in short, to try to contribute to this. With these budgets, ladies and gentlemen, Spain will move forward and, with them, the Spanish and the Spanish. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u7	Thank you, President. Madam Minister, indeed, you have said, European funds are a very important part of this budget, they are a very important part, and it is also true - and that must be acknowledged - that part of these European funds will be implemented through channelling through the autonomous communities and local corporations. We have reached an agreement with the Government to make it possible for the four Members of the Democratic Party to join in the approval of these budgets in relation to a very specific aspect, and it is that of the funds channelled through the autonomous communities. I would like to thank the Government for being sensitive not to giving Catalonia any privileges, but to recognizing the uniqueness in the provision of certain public services in Catalonia. Catalonia, as you know, has a system for the provision of public services in the field of health and of the various very peculiar social services and actions, based on public-private collaboration. Many non-profit entities, which are part of what we call the third sector in Catalonia, provide these services. The paradoxical situation was that in the first implementation of these funds in the field of health, in the field of social services, it was not possible for these non-profit entities, but providing public services, to have access to these funds of the European Union which were reserved essentially for public investment. That the investments must be public had a reason to be if the public service only provided it by the public administration. In Catalonia we found that on various occasions there were non-profit institutions which were the only ones providing public service in the field of health or 'in a social sphere, such as the care of persons with disabilities, etc., who could not access these funds, and we have reached an agreement with the Government whereby the non-profit entities providing public services in the field of health and in the field of social services will also be able to access these calls, these lines to finance the investments of their equipment. It makes no sense - that was happening at the moment in Catalonia - for example, that reference institutions in the field of health, such as the Hospital de Sant Joan de Déu, a reference in maternal and child health in the public health services of Catalonia, cannot access these funds to make some extension or improvement of the hospital itself or - I give you another example - that the Hospital de El Vendrell, which is managed by the Fundación Sant Pau i Santa Tecla, in Tarragona, cannot access these funds to carry out an extension of the hospital when all public health in the Baix Penedès region is provided by these types of entities. We are therefore pleased that you have been sensitive to this reality. If someone tries to explain to them that this is a privilege for Catalonia, do not be deceived; simply, it is to adapt the possibility of applying these funds to reality in Catalonia. This should also be an example for other cases and situations. If some of you - you know that is not my case - want to maintain a plurinational State, you must make it possible for the State to adapt to that plurinationality. And plurinationality is not only language, but in different areas our societies have evolved differently. And if more than 30% of the provision of public health and social services in Catalonia is carried out through these non-profit institutions, it is also fair for them to have access to European funds. I therefore thank you for this change of approach; rather than a change of approach, it is an adaptation of the approach to a reality that was perhaps not sufficiently known, but which is very important. This will make it possible for us to vote in favour of these budgets, together, of course, with other amendments. Thank you so much.

[[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 23, Ministry for Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge We move on to the debate in section 23, Ministry for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the third Vice-President and Minister for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u21	President, ladies and gentlemen, good morning. I rise to this rostrum to defend the draft State General Budgets Act concerning the section of the Ministry for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge. I am grateful for the certainty provided by the adoption of the General Budgets of the State, as they represent clear support for the ecological transition policies demanded by a broad social majority and by members of this House; they strengthen economic recovery; they strengthen the sense of social justice linked to the protection of our resources; and they facilitate the fulfilment of our international commitments, both with regard to the climate and with regard to the Sustainable Development Goals. The budget as a whole amounts to EUR 10 195 million, of which EUR 5817 million is from the national budget - which has grown from EUR 5500 in 2021 - and EUR 4378 million is from the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, which together with the rest is an unprecedented investment figure. I would like to point out that, on 15 November, in this implementation of the 2021 budget, we are close to 70%, and I trust that the year will end with a figure close to 90% with regard to the resources of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. This budget he presented represents an unprecedented mobilization in relation to three of the country's major challenges: the transformation of the energy system, the response to the climate emergency and the protection of biodiversity and territorial cohesion, inclusion and equality with our compatriots living in small municipalities. The first major challenge, related to the energy transition, has a budget of 7397 million, of which 4500 correspond to the regular budget and more than 2800 come from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Mechanism. The transfers of the Secretariat of State are more than 4 billion, of which 3695 correspond to the National Commission on Markets and Competition and will be to contribute from the General Budgets of the State, with a large part of what is received from Europe for revenues of auctions of CO2 rights, to the fixed costs of the electricity system at a particularly sensitive time. To them are added 716 million to finance the extra cost of energy in island territories. In other words, both headings are linked to key elements from the point of view of solidarity with consumers. From a substantive point of view, a key factor in the energy transition is that of the pace of implementation of renewable energies, so 435 million are earmarked for the Innovative Renewable Energy Development Plan, integrated in building and productive processes - i.e. component 2 of the Recovery Plan - and another 175 million for the sustainable energy investment line on the islands, of component 7 of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. But if there is something essential in any deep, intense and rapid transformation as it is that we are already living - we like it or we like it, and fortunately we have been working on it for a long time - it is always thinking about the social dimension and the redistributive effects, about the concerns that workers and consumers may feel at complex and turbulent times like this. That is why we are making this transition a transition that we want to adjust with workers, neighbours and regions. Citizens and territories whose economies were based on fossil fuels and their associated economic activity should not bear the social and economic costs of such transformation, let alone do so alone. A dynamic society must ensure solidarity mechanisms and this is what we do with the budget allocation for the Institute for the Just Transition, which will grow to 442 million, including 110 in transfers from the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. The Institute's budget highlights the following programmes: 53 million for employment-generating projects, 4 million for social development projects, 140 for payments to coal mining workers, including pre-retirement, low compensation and employability programmes, and 85 million for agreements with autonomous communities. In the same section, an additional 10 million are allocated for the restoration of mining areas and 15.7 for the closure plan for mining and electricity companies. Of the 110 million of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, the focus is primarily on environmental restoration, the infrastructure plan, energy R & D+i projects and a support plan for retraining and job reintegration. They are also actions that have forty-one million from the European Union's Fair Transition Fund. Nor can we forget that recovery must be fair to consumers, especially to the most vulnerable - I mentioned earlier - at a time when the payment of energy supplies is vital. Our commitment to energy poverty is maintained and strengthened. We want to strengthen it in this complicated year that we live in. I would like to highlight the EUR 157 million item for the thermal social bond, which is increased by 44% over the 2021 figures with the intention of catering to those groups with the greatest difficulties in dealing with the energy bill, the most vulnerable groups. It is a matter of elementary justice for our fellow citizens. Among the challenges facing Spain are improving the manageability of renewables in the system. Therefore, storage policies are key, must take off and lift the flight. We have allocated 289 million precisely to storage. It will be necessary to continue to budget for actions in various areas, including the hydrogen route, which will have 555 million for renewable hydrogen programmes, including the associated industrial value chain. These are actions of components 9 and 8 of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, respectively. Electricity, energy efficiency and renewables are the three fundamental axes for success in the transformation of the energy system. Therefore, the budgetary effort made for the electrification of transport as an indispensable part of this transition is essential. It is a shared effort with other ministerial departments, but in our case it represents a 445 million allocation for the Plan of Incentives for the installation of charging points, the acquisition of electric vehicles and fuel cells and the innovation in electromobility, charging and green hydrogen applied to mobility. They are projects that give continuity to the lines started and executed in the year 2021. Measures for energy efficiency, self-consumption and the promotion of energy communities are a real success; they are the pillar, the basis of transformation, they serve to reduce reliance on markets or large operators; they serve to reduce energy consumption; they serve to lower citizens' bills; they serve to support cooperatives, to support small trade, to support small industry, but also small municipalities. For this reason, these policies will have €300 million to be financed from ERDF funds for the period 2021-2027 and managed by IDAE, Institute for Diversification and Energy Saving, which also has an additional €12 million for the digitisation of the electricity sector, €6 million for innovation and value chain and €10 million for social measures in the energy sector, in addition to the other €20 million in component 7 of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, which will strengthen the energy community's momentum. In this area, we included $434.7 million for the Energy Transition Plan in the General State Administration, component 11 of the plan. From the Programme of regeneration and demographic challenge there will be 350 million destined for urban rehabilitation and regeneration in local entities less than 5000 inhabitants, and for the Programme sustainable urban development, DUS 5000, which has had great success during the opening period started just ten days ago and still open. As you can see, the energy effort of these budgets is enormously relevant and will serve to meet the challenge of the transition to a clean, efficient, sustainable energy that takes into account the territories and above all people, with criteria of social justice and equal opportunities in sustainable development. The second major challenge we face is the preservation of our ecosystems, biodiversity, natural resources and territory influenced by anthropogenic action and also by the severe consequences of climate change and global warming. The Secretariat of State for the Environment and its Directorates-General and subordinate bodies have a budget of EUR 579 million, 103 more than in 2021 in their regular budget, representing an additional 2.85%. Of these, 1502 million come from the Recovery Plan and 2077 million from the regular funding to which it referred. Within the Secretariat of State for the Environment, the Directorate-General for Biodiversity, Forests and Desertification will have a budget of $517.7 million, 174 from the regular budget, plus 343 from the Recovery Plan. The budget for the preservation of biodiversity amounts to 60.13 million when we talk about terrestrial biodiversity. This is why we want to finance a number of actions, but also, of course, to strengthen support, support for farmers and the local administration in order to prevent and facilitate conservation and coexistence with the wolf and large carnivores. In total, an amount of EUR 20 million financed for the first time by the Government of Spain, with the conviction that this is the response consistent with the national dimension for the protection of this species. The conservation of a species and its coexistence with livestock cannot be an exclusive responsibility nor must its cost be borne by farmers; it cannot be a burden for a few. It is everyone's responsibility, it is therefore the responsibility of these budgets, which contain an amount that is greater than the amount that has been allocated annually to the autonomous communities that have the presence of the wolf. The chapter for the preservation of marine biodiversity has a budget of 42,2 million euros. The sea must worry about many reasons: it is one of the main sinks of CO2, it has a fundamental thermostat, it has an immense wealth partly unknown and we know that it has enormous threats to face. As oceanographers like to say, without blue, there's no way there's green. Investment programmes will also be carried out on lines for the digitization and improvement of knowledge of the natural environment, amounting to 34.28 million; for the restoration of ecosystems, with 113 million, among which we include some of the most important projects in some of the most emblematic places in our territory, such as the framework of priority actions for the Minor Sea and the restoration of mining areas in this space. Sustainable forest management is funded with 116 million euros and 135 more from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. The extinction of forest fires, reinforcing and accompanying the competence of the autonomous communities, has a budget of 118 million. As you know, the increase in fire risk caused by climate change is exponential. Our means, the anticipation, the strengthening of the services of extinction and Civil Protection is an indisputable priority in the Mediterranean area. It is also so in our country, which has exceptional professionals. In the area of water resources, the Directorate-General for Water will have a budget of EUR 2627 million. The main lines of this budget are intended to increase the level of investment, to maintain support for hydrographic confederations and to bet decisively on the procedures that the European Union requires us to modernise, manage, monitor and sanction, fulfilling the obligations of the Water Framework Directive, also promoting innovation and the development of the perhaps more precious and more complex good, generating social welfare and economic wealth in our country. For this reason, we will invest 505 million, 250 million from the regular budget and 255 million from the Recovery Plan, with emblematic projects in different cities in Spain, responding and accompanying the competent administrations - local and autonomous - in a challenge that unfortunately still keeps us with infringement procedures and enforcement fines at European level. In the restoration and conservation of the hydraulic public domain and defense against floods will be reached 237 million regular budget and 344.6 million of the Recovery Plan, to which will have to be added 917 million managed by the autonomous agencies, that is, the hydrographic confederations of the ministry. Prevention against the risk of flooding, investment, management and the ability to have a resilient response to extreme weather events - which we have unfortunately been accustomed to - is essential. Investments aimed at promoting the National Strategy for the Restoration of Rivers or nature-based solutions such as protection mechanisms, the recovery of shoreline vegetation and channel morphology have a budget of 103.5 million. The security, maintenance and maintenance of infrastructure has a program of 74.5 million, as well as the digital transition in the water sector, capital for the good management and minimization of impacts, both quantitative and qualitative, with 105 million investments, prioritizing sensorization to increase knowledge and better management and use of water resources. The Directorate-General of the Coast and Sea has a total of 190.1 million, 95 from the regular budget and 95 from the Recovery Plan. Again, adaptation to climate change and increased resilience are essential factors in planning actions against erosion, coastal strengthening and the recovery of emblematic spaces, some of which are greatly degraded in the maritime-terrestrial public domain. In this area we will have 10.5 million in recovery of degraded spaces and 8.5 million for improvements in accessibility to the maritime-terrestrial public domain. Within the investments aimed at preserving the territory and restoring ecosystems, I would like to pause for a moment in the budget of the Autonomous National Parks Agency. We have budgeted 47.2 million, 7 million more than in 2021, including investments for the new parks; parks, gentlemen, which you have already supported with your vote, such as the Sierra de las Nieves National Park or the extension of the Maritime-Terrestrial National Park of the Cabrera Archipelago. As you know, the Secretariat of State for the Environment is responsible for environmental assessment policies and the impetus of the circular economy, perhaps one of the most important axes of industrial modernization and the economy in our country. This Directorate-General has a budget of EUR 310 million, which will be earmarked for two major items: the implementation of waste regulations - in processing - and the promotion of the circular economy, with transfers to the 175 million autonomous communities, and in the area of companies with EUR 124.5 million, and we will have important initiatives that we hope will properly develop the law on contaminated waste and soil, in which you are working. The Spanish Climate Change Office has 19.43 million euros, highlighting the contribution to the Carbon Fund with 12.8 million euros, from where we fund innovative projects for the reduction of CO2 emissions. The autonomous bodies attached to the Secretariat of State have a budget of 250 million, which will allow us to finance the actions of the hydrographic confederations, the water community of Taibilla, the Spanish Meteorology Agency and 10.5 million for the Biodiversity Foundation. This challenge of transformation, preservation of biodiversity and response to the climate emergency we face with extraordinary budgetary tools. We believe that protecting our biodiversity, natural resources, the drive of the Green Infrastructure Strategy, the protection of water, territory, coasts, sea, natural parks and betting on the circular economy is essential. The third challenge I referred to at the beginning of my intervention, no less important, is the so-called demographic challenge. We believe that this is a question of the quality of democracy; we believe that this is a key issue in terms of social cohesion in the territory. We believe that it is essential to promote and develop policies for territorial and social cohesion in Spain. This is a cross-cutting policy, involving the commitment of all levels of political action, including all ministerial departments. We want to place special emphasis on measures accompanying young people and women, on the most innovative elements, new technologies and connectivity, as well as strengthening and innovation in the provision of services. Social development, equal access to public services, the generation of rural economies, the gender perspective and the presence of the State in the territory are some of the approaches we have been working on. That is why, in addition to talking about the budget allocated to the General Secretariat, I would like once again to highlight the 130 measures for the demographic challenge, which have a budget of 10 billion since 2021 and of which more than 70% have started their course. It would be pointless to pursue a population challenge policy that did not have this cross-cutting approach on the Government as a whole and on the institutions as a whole. That is why, together with the more than 4200 million announced by the President of the Government, we want to concentrate some of the elements that convey the most important actions in the budget of the General Secretariat of the Challenge. We know that among these headings there are some significant reasons why they represent innovators or testing alternative measures, innovative proposals, especially for young people and women, he said earlier. We have EUR 55.79 million to activate, strengthen the initiatives associated with the deployment of the rural field, cohesion and territorial transformation, the search for innovative projects for territorial transformation and the fight against depopulation, the rural impulse and the generation of new ecosystems through centres of intelligence and territorial innovation, as well as the promotion of measures that allow us to better understand the fields of knowledge and prioritisation in the field of inequalities and territorial response. These are three major challenges to which this vice-presidency and ministry have decided to face, three major challenges in relation to which we hope to be able to count on their support, not only through support for budgets, but also through the interest they show in all these initiatives in their territories, in those demarcations from which they come, in those industrial, economic and social spaces with which they work. The bed is the budgets, but not only; his will, his day-to-day work is decisive. I would therefore like to point out that these budgets, these proposals, are certainly fundamental tools to move towards the goal of a fair recovery, a recovery that will enable our economy to be modernised, that will allow greater social justice, that will enable our young people to move towards the future they deserve. [[The lady]] These budgets for 2022 will undoubtedly contribute to making Spain a more productive, more competitive, more sustainable, more just, more environment-friendly country, to ensuring prosperity for all families within the limits of our planet. These are budgets that will make it possible to move towards a green, digital, gender-free, cohesive and inclusive Spain. That is why, as María Zambrano would say, we do not move from the real to the real, but from the impossible to the real, and with these budgets we make true the ecological transition policies that were not even on the radar of this House a few years ago. ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u23	Thank you, President. Madam Minister, ladies and gentlemen, after yesterday's closing of a final fringe of the negotiation of the budgets, we say that we will support them in their entirety. And we support them for our commitment to the citizens who need the resources of the State and the European Union, so that services, investments and funds can reach businesses, families, individuals, administrations and boost economic recovery. We support them to facilitate the implementation of the European funds, which must mobilise impact projects for the whole of Spain, but above all we support them to promote the first progress in the fight for the territorial balance contained in these budgets and, therefore, for the recognition of the need for a new, just territorial policy with the territories of the emptied Spain. We will be very vigilant with the implementation of the budget and with the commitments made, among which we would like to highlight the EUR 4200 million earmarked for the demographic challenge, which must be effective in its implementation in order to have the desired effect. And we will also be vigilant in the development and implementation of aid for the operation of companies, which includes additional provision 115, for sparsely populated territories, such as those in Cuenca, Soria and Teruel - as the European Community itself acknowledges - and those adjacent territories with a population density of less than 12.5 inhabitants per square kilometre. In section 23, we have tabled 24 amendments concerning all these aspects. The themes of the projects in this section are economic issues, but they are also central to shaping a new territorial policy. And for us they are not precisely because of the search for territorial advantages for unpopulated areas, but rather because of the need to combat depopulation and its effects wherever it has occurred, because of the need to address the demographic challenge and because of the need to achieve a fair energy transition with the territories that have suffered the impact of mining and the closure of coal thermal power stations. It seems logical that all these issues should be considered in the General Budgets of the State. Similarly, budgets must contribute to a well-regulated and orderly deployment of renewable energies; a deployment of renewables strictly respectful of the rules for the care of the environment, the landscape and the territory; a deployment of renewable energies that contributes to the development of the territory, to promote and distribute wealth through a distributed generation model and, consequently, also to enable the fight against depopulation. In short, with the amendments to section 23, we are trying to ensure that the territories are populated, to reduce the demographic deserts and, therefore, to start reversing the territorial imbalances. The environment concerns us because the environmental crisis has global dimensions and because the Government of Spain and the governments of the autonomous communities are not - in our opinion - taking the necessary decisive steps to prevent the environmental catastrophe that threatens many societies and territories. We are concerned that the climate emergency requires the commitment of society as a whole and more rigorous actions in the protection of the environment, including its socio-economic dimension, agreed between the administrations, as we have advocated, for example, in the Climate Change Act, including the management of a deployment of renewables that is currently out of control and over the micro-centres. On many occasions it is forgotten that the territory - with its physical and environmental variables - is the basic resource of societies and the product of the interaction between society and the environment. It is forgotten that taking care of the territory, as an indispensable resource, is the initial premise of a necessary ecological transition. Consequently, territorial balance is a substantial and indissoluble part of the transition, which is the name and objective of this budget section. Achieving the ecological transition must mean developing the potential of each territory. For this reason, much of our amendments in this and other sections of the budget are part of the fight against territorial imbalances and against depopulation. He said that we have tabled twenty-four amendments to the text of this section, some of which I would like to highlight, for example, amendments specifically aimed at combating depopulation and territorial imbalances through a very diverse set of proposals. Amendment 21, for example, proposes the creation of a State agency against depopulation; 110, promote a study leading to the deconcentration of public enterprises and administrative centres in sparsely populated provinces; 111, analyse the creation of multi-activity offices of the General Administration of the State in sparsely populated areas; 112, promote tractor projects in areas lacking sufficient initiatives and promote them by the Administration itself; and Amendment 29, for the installation of multi-entity ATMs in small municipalities that have lost it. A second group of amendments is aimed at managing water resources by means of small storage, regulation, restoration of river resources or improvement of culverts in the Mediterranean or inland basins. Thus, Amendment 37 proposes the creation of side rafts in the Matarraña river; 104 proposes a reversible water jump to serve as a battery of energy storage between Alcañiz and Samper de Calanda; 40 is carried out for the current Mequinenza-Civán pumping, by converting the installation to photovoltaic energy and the execution of high regulating rafts; 103, for the project of remodeling the dam of the Calanda swamp in the Guadalope river. Another group of amendments focus on the energy transition. 108 proposes to boost energy efficiency in agricultural cooperatives. 109, the valorization of public and private mountains and agricultural areas in the capture of CO2. In turn, with regard to the energy transition, we have tabled an amendment to the article of another kind. It is a proposal for an additional provision that did not entail expenditure. Amendment 117 proposes the creation of a physical model for the deployment of renewable energies for the management of the deployment of power generation facilities through the necessary legislative planning and development tools. It is about ordering the accelerated deployment of renewables, which is clearly focused on the construction of micro-centres without considering local communities, without regard to environmental and landscape values and without addressing the territory in which they are located. The amendment seeks precisely that distributed generation and self-consumption cease to have marginal treatment, as is the case in the current energy transition policy, to play a relevant role in ensuring that this energy change benefits broad sections of the population. The examples can be Germany or Denmark and we cannot walk in the opposite direction. I would also like to highlight a group of amendments which concern matters falling within the competence of this section, such as the care of the forest and the natural environment, which, although they do not involve high and low in the headings, were budgetary amendments. Its implementation would have succeeded in increasing income through taxes. I am referring, for example, to Amendments 113, 114 and 115 to regularise and update the ownership and functioning of partner farms, amendments that seek to activate and regularise 1.5 million hectares of forests and mountains, a huge extension that would allow the activity and resources of unpopulated areas to be increased. I would like to conclude by recalling that achieving the ecological transition means developing the potential of each territory, which in this section 23 are many of the springs that we must mobilize to combat depopulation and to reverse them territorial slopes, that a balanced territory is an essential condition for greater respect for the environment, for a more rational management of resources and for a better quality of life.

[[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 24, Culture and Sport We continue with the debate in section 24, Ministry of Culture and Sport. For the presentation of the section, the Minister for Culture and Sport has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u51	Thank you very much and good morning, President. Ladies and gentlemen, from the satisfaction of the broad parliamentary agreement reached and with the thanks to the groups that have made it possible, I rise to the rostrum to defend the draft Law on General State Budgets as regards section 24, referred to by the Ministry of Culture and Sport. As I have already had occasion to express on several occasions, from the government we have conceived the budget as a key tool for the rebirth of culture and sport in Spain. The budget is an important lever for achieving this objective. Culture and sport are a framework of rights, but they are also part of our system of values, built throughout history and which defines us as a society and as a country. In addition, they constitute an industry that generates wealth and employment, with enormous growth potential both nationally and internationally, and can and should be an important strategic engine for economic and employment recovery. As the Minister of Finance and Public Service, María Jesús Montero, pointed out in her appearance, this Government wants the world of culture to have in these accounts the opportunity to take a new impulse and be a motor, too, of recovery and dynamism. Culture and sport budgets must be an engine for accelerating the modernization of the productive fabric of the cultural and sports sector. They must promote the growth of this industry, productivity and competitiveness of these sectors. They must generate jobs and help overcome the precarious economic situation in which professionals and companies in these sectors move. And that's the project I'm presenting to you today. The overall figure of EUR 1589 million which the Spanish Government will allocate to cultural and sports policies represents an increase of 38,4 % compared to this year's, and is, ladies and gentlemen, the highest budget in the historical series. This figure represents EUR 364 million from the European funds of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, which we will distribute to the autonomous communities to carry out policies aimed at promoting culture and sport. Even without this investment of European funds, the budget for the year 2022 in culture is the highest since 2009. They are ambitious budgets that aim to contribute to a fair recovery, to social reconstruction, to social and territorial vertebration and cohesion, and to the cultural and sporting renaissance that must take place in 2022. We have two fundamental objectives: the first, supporting the industrial fabric and contributing to its recovery after the pandemic; and the second, promoting cultural and sporting habits and creating new audiences, especially among the youngest. In short, it is about getting out of the crisis stronger, more creative, more prepared, with more culture and more sport. With this ambition, the budgets envisage for the first time an item of 210 million euros destined for the young cultural bonus. We estimate that a total of approximately half a million young Spaniards, those who are eighteen years old throughout 2022, will be able to benefit from a bonus of 400 euros for the consumption of cultural products and activities. The two main objectives of the cultural bonus are: first, to facilitate the access of younger audiences to culture and to revitalize and boost the cultural industry following the effects of the pandemic on the sector; and, secondly, to promote the retention of the habit of consuming cultural products among young people, so that as adults they continue to consume cultural products assiduously. The ministry has carried out a detailed study of the application of this type of initiative in other countries such as France and Italy and we are designing the Spanish bonus, which will establish mechanisms for young people to know and consume different types of experiences and cultural products: concerts, performing arts, cinema, books, museums, etc. The cultural bonus is further proof that government accounts provide strong support to young people, and we also seek to revitalize and revitalize the industries and cultural sectors most affected by the pandemic. I would also like to highlight the important commitment to strengthen the staff at the disposal of the Ministry of Culture and Sport in order to be able to manage better, so as to be able to offer the best service to citizens. In particular, EUR 7 million more has been planned than in 2021. This investment responds to the increased supply of public employment for culture in recent decades. This will make it possible to increase the incorporation of civil servants into the bodies of archivists, librarians and conservators of the State. A total of around five hundred new employees and public employees will be incorporated. As I pointed out at the beginning, we have the will to relaunch the cultural sector, to promote cultural rebirth, so necessary after the toughest moments of the pandemic. A clear example of this desire is the film section, which will have 97 million euros, about 10 million more than this year, adding aid to support cinemas and also fulfilling the mandate of Article 3 of the Spanish Constitution, which mandates us to protect all the languages of Spain. On the other hand, the performing arts, dance, circus and live music, managed by the National Institute of Performing Arts, will have a budget of 171 million euros, 8.8 million more than in 2021. This will allow us to expand competitive competition aids aimed at boosting theatre, circus, dance, lyric and music. Another sector that will see its economic endowment increased is the book and reading sector. The objective is to consolidate the increase in reading experienced during the pandemic. We want citizens to continue reading, to read more. Thus, the budget allocated to the book in 2022 and reading is increased by 13% compared to 2021 and reaches a total of EUR 22 821 000. Among the projects to be financed are the acquisition of digital books for the eBible application, which doubles to EUR 1 400 000, and the internationalization and promotion of the Spanish book and letters, which increases by 19%. We also increase activities and grants for the promotion of reading. We created for the first time aid to local authorities for the promotion of reading with EUR 1 400 000. Ladies and gentlemen, as you already know, on 24 October Spain took over Canada as a guest country at the Frankfurt Book Fair. Thus, next year, thirty-one years after our participation in 1991, we held the rank of guest of honor at the Book Fair of Frankfurt 2022, the most important international publishing sector, where 80% of global copyright is negotiated. The recovery budgets also have greater allocations for cultural institutions. Thus, the Prado National Museum has 9.7 million more than in 2012. Of these, 2.2 million will be for temporary exhibitions and another 7 will be for the necessary rehabilitation and museum adaptation works of the Kingdom Hall. For its part, the budget of the Museo Nacional Centro de Arte Reina Sofía has 4 million euros more; this increase will be destined for the acquisition of works of contemporary art and with this we also intend to promote the production of art in our country. As for the Fundación Colección Thyssen-Bornemisza, its endowment is increased by 11 million euros, which will be destined for the conservation, study, public exhibition and dissemination of the Thyssen-Bornemisza collection, in which also works of the Carmen Thyssen-Bornemisza collection are exhibited. For its part, the National Library of Spain has an additional 1.2 million euros, an additional 4.48%, with which it will face an increase in exhibitions and the improvement of library services. These budgets are very territorial; in other words, they have a strong presence in Spain as a whole, from the rural area to the large cities and capitals of our geography. In the case of Madrid, for example, the territorialised expenditure for 2022 amounts to 54 million euros, 21.5 million euros more than the previous year, representing an increase of 66.25% over 2021. If we consider the investments in the Museo Nacional del Prado, 55,6 million euros, plus those of the Reina Sofia Art Center, 42,3 million euros, plus those of the National Library, 32,2 million euros, the total investment of the Government of Spain in cultural institutions in the Community of Madrid is 184,1 million euros. To this should be added an important part of the 171 million budgeted in the National Institute of Performing Arts and Music, and I refer to those corresponding to the National Drama Centre, the Royal Theatre, the Teatro de la Zarzuela, the National Auditorium of Music, the National Music Dissemination Centre, the National Classical Theatre Company, the Spanish National Ballet and the National Dance Company, all of which, by the way, have permanent headquarters in Madrid. You know that there are those who say that this government neglects Madrid, but I believe that the figures clearly deny that fear or that accusation. I would also like to stress that we have once again included, one more year, the EUR 20 million earmarked for Barcelona's cultural capital. The aim is nothing other than to recover and intensify the State's cultural and scientific relationship with Barcelona. Madrid and Barcelona are clearly and clearly two of the main Spanish scientific cultural capitals, in addition to two of the main European capitals in these areas. We must take advantage of the fact that together Madrid and Barcelona, Barcelona and Madrid are unbeatable and that by strengthening the cultural and scientific heritage and wealth of these two cities all of Spain is gaining. [[Applauds]] It also has to do with the territorialization of budgets the allocation for investments in the buildings of Culture, both those of direct management by the ministry and when it has been transferred to the autonomous communities. In this respect, budgets are increased by EUR 7 million, to EUR 43 million. This represents an increase of almost 20% over the 2021 accounts. Let me give you some examples: the adequacy of the former convent of the Conception for the headquarters of the Archivo de Burgos, the demolition of the existing building in the building for the future public library of the State in Barcelona, the rehabilitation of the public library of the State in Valladolid, the works of adaptation of the former convent of San Agustín for the headquarters of the public library of the State in Malaga, the works of rehabilitation of the public library of the State in Alicante, the works of rehabilitation of the Museum of the Wall in Mallorca, the expansion of the National Museum of Roman Art in Mérida, the rehabilitation of the Museum of Caceres, the expansion of the Sorolla Museum in Madrid, the renovation and rehabilitation of the Palace of the Eagles in Ávila and the rehabilitation of the Archaeological Museum in Ourense. Ladies and gentlemen, I should like to conclude my speech on the cultural sector by stressing that the cultural policy of the current ministerial team and of this Government does not include any plans for the transfer of works in national museums. I say this because the words are carried by the wind, but the budget lines are there. And for the record: there is no decision, no intention, no budget item, no future plan that will diminish the role of national museums or that will affect the integrity of their collections. [[Applauds]] On the contrary, the efforts are focused on implementing collections, concessions or new acquisitions, such as the purchase of the Crucifixion painting of El Greco - as published in the State Official Gazette last Friday, 19-, which will be destined for the collection of the Museum of the Greco, in Toledo. This painting, by the way, will be the first work of the artist to enter the museum's funds since it was founded in 1910. I repeat, this will be the first work to be added to the museum's funds since 1910. In any case, I take the opportunity to recall that national museums belong to the whole of the citizenry, regardless of their specific location, which is why they are called national museums. [[Applauds]] As far as sport is concerned, I would like to point out that the EUR 315 million earmarked for sport represents the largest investment in sport in history: EUR 208 million from the regular budget and EUR 107 million from the recovery plan. If we take into account that in the 2021 budgets there were extra items to cover the Olympic and Paralympic Games, this 25% increase means, in real terms, a consolidation and a stake in sport as an engine for the economy and society. With regard to the regular budget of the Higher Sports Council, there has been an increase of 7 million, five of which are earmarked for the launch of the women's professional football league. As you can see, we are also strengthening the equality policies to which we are committed by the Government. The Sectoral Conference on Sport, held last 8 November, approved the allocation of 75 million among the autonomous communities to modernize fifty-four high-performance sports centres and sports technology, including the financing of actions aimed at replacing the current energies they use in their operation with others with lower pollutant emissions, as well as the renovation of equipment to achieve the maximum energy efficiency of each of the systems available to them. It is a series of actions that align with the ecological transition objectives contained in the Government-driven Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. For its part, of the funds from the recovery funds, EUR 31.8 million will be allocated entirely to the Social Plan for Sport, which includes projects for equality, inclusion and social sports policies. Regular investments in sports federations increase to EUR 55 886 750, an increase of almost EUR 3 million over the previous year. A million and a half euros will be earmarked for sports technology and promotion programmes. These are, in short, budgets for further promoting social cohesion in sport. Similarly, there is an investment of EUR 2.55 million for sport and women, social aid for women sportsmen, with EUR 400 000; aid to federations with inclusive sports projects, with EUR 750,000, and aid to federations for sports activities of school age is increased by more than 50%, as well as aid to the autonomous communities, with a new allocation for equipment for technology centres with EUR 1 million. Ladies and gentlemen, as I pointed out before, we are faced with very ambitious budgets that must bring about a revival of culture and sport after the pandemic. I hope that, in this commitment of the Government to boost culture and sport, the various political groups will agree that, with the support I ask your ladies and gentlemen to these budgets and specifically to this section, we will be able to promote the cultural and sporting revival that Spanish society demands and deserves. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

As you know, the Ministry of the Presidency, Relations with the Courts and Democratic Memory is not an investor ministry and our departures are intended to support the Head of State, to support the Presidency of the Government, to coordinate between ministries and to develop sectoral policies in the field of democratic memory and religious freedom, and we also provide funding, as it cannot otherwise be, to the public agencies that are attached to this ministry. The total consolidated budget of the Ministry for 2022 amounts to 188.65 million euros, i.e. 7.8 more than in the previous year. The items dedicated to democratic memory amount to EUR 11.82 million, an increase of around 4%. Sixty per cent of this budget will be devoted to locating, identifying and giving a decent burial to people who have been victims of the Civil War or the dictatorship. It is a humanitarian; humanitarian policy. [[Applauds]] We also target specific items to dignify, resignify and create memory places. There is also an item in our budget and in our economic effort to develop competences in the area of religious freedom. In particular, the party dedicated to the Fundación Pluralismo y Convivencia - which you will know of - whose endowment is maintained at 1.9 million euros and which serves to provide funding to the different religious confessions that live together in our country. There is little news in the Government Presidency's expenditure programme. Those that exist are the result of the 2% increase agreed for all public workers, the need to conduct cybersecurity studies and the reactivation of the international agenda of the President of the Government who, after overcoming the pandemic, will resume with normality with the international and European vocation that the President of the Government has. We also support the Head of State from the Ministry of the Presidency, where the budget, which is not significantly changed, amounts to €7.22 million in the case of the Royal House. As for the programmes that finance the Ministry's common services, the total amount amounts to €37.5 million, an increase of €1.96 million, also motivated by the increase in salaries to public workers and employees, to officials, the hiring of a cafeteria and dining service for the workers of the complex and the investment, always necessary, in computer technology and in adapting computer equipment to reality. Finally, I would like to refer to the three autonomous agencies attached to the ministry and the agency attached to it, which is also the agency of the BOE. A total of €96 million is to be added to the Ministry's consolidated budget of €188.6 million. The BOE budget remains unchanged. The increase of 2 million euros is due to the need to undertake reforms in some of the buildings. ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u107	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I appear before this House for the defence of section 26 of the State General Budgets Bill for the year 2022, concerning the Ministry of Health. Let me first make a brief reference to the current situation of the pandemic in Spain, since, according to the latest data, we have seen indicators of average risk levels, with a sustained increase in the cumulative incidence located - yesterday's data - to 139 to fourteen days, without, so far - said cautiously - a significant reflection on the aid pressure. It is a differentiated situation from the countries in our environment, which have very high figures in all indicators, which reflect an evolution of the pandemic and a very low percentage of vaccination compared to what we have in our country. As you know, in Spain we have a very high vaccination rate, around 90%, which we have achieved within the framework of our National Health System and co-governance, and thanks in particular to our healthcare professionals and exemplary citizenship, who soon understood and understood the magnitude of their responsibility and came to the call of vaccination. Ladies and gentlemen, a few days ago the Director-General of WHO in Europe asked us to document the lessons learned during the COVID-19 pandemic because we are a reference for the rest of the countries within and outside the Union. We are, too, because this Government has firmly committed itself to solidarity with other peoples. We have moved to action with the determined leadership in the international donation of vaccines, because from this pandemic either we all leave or no one leaves. We announced weeks ago that we will donate 50 million doses by the first quarter of 2022 and we can confirm that, today, we have delivered more than 30 million doses to the Covax mechanism. Spain therefore fulfils and so does this Government. We are the fifth country in the world to deliver solidary vaccines, ladies and gentlemen; we have fulfilled and progressed. [[Applauds]] There is also an important milestone, ladies and gentlemen, which will allow me to say this this afternoon, and it is that this commitment of solidarity of the Government of Spain also interferes with the universal vocation of science in improving the lives of all people. This is the case with the assignment of a diagnostic test license to the VOCID-19 developed entirely by the CSIC and which will enable it to reach the countries most in need at the hands of a United Nations initiative, Medicines Patent Pool. These Spanish label serological tests will also be the first diagnostic technology to be included in the C-TAP, an instrument launched by the World Health Organization to facilitate access to VOCID technologies in any country that requests it through non-exclusive and transparent licenses. This is, ladies and gentlemen, commitment and solidarity. I now turn, ladies and gentlemen, to the figures in section 26. They are budgets aimed at consolidating the just economic and social recovery advocated by the Spanish Government, and they also seek to guarantee the vaccines we need with two fundamental objectives. The first objective, ladies and gentlemen, is to continue to advance in the fairness, universality and cohesion of the National Health System. It is, therefore, a question of shielding one of the fundamental pillars of the welfare state and, in addition, it is a question of continuing to strengthen its capacities to meet present and future health challenges. As regards the overall figures for the consolidated budget, i.e. once internal transfers have been compensated, the allocation for the financial year 2022, including its autonomous seconded bodies, amounts to EUR 2546 million, which includes both the national budget and that for the implementation of projects financed from European funds. If we focus, ladies and gentlemen, on the State subsector, without including the European funds, the budget for the year 2022 amounts to EUR 1171 million in the consolidated budget. This figure, compared to the budget for 2021, means that the department will receive an increase of 1028 million euros by 2022. Ladies and gentlemen, after commenting on the overall figures, I am now going to comment briefly on the figures in the various areas of the department. In public health, the budget is 1739 million, including European funds that will be allocated to key projects such as the purchase of vaccines against COVID-19 with 1463 million, of which 292 million euros are destined to purchase solidary vaccines. They are also going to go to primary and community care, with a budget close to € 176 million for the year 2022, of which the vast majority -172 million - are going to be distributed to the autonomous communities. The aim is to contribute to the development in the short term of the action plan for strengthening primary care in which we are working within the institutional technical committee and which will be ready by the end of 2021. Also, ladies and gentlemen, an important action under this heading is the Mental Health Action Plan and COVID-19, to deal with the urgency of the important, which has a budget for the year 2022 of 30 million euros, of which 24 will be transferred to the autonomous communities and the rest will be allocated to a telephone of 24 hours seven days a week for the prevention and care of suicidal behaviour.
