ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u127	Thank you very much, President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. I stand before this House today to defend section 27 of the State General Budgets Bill for 2022. But before going into the details of this section, I would like to take a moment on the eve of the final vote in Congress to share with you the importance of these budgets to consolidate economic recovery and social recovery. The values that support the General Budgets of the State for 2022 are those that have been guiding our Government's economic policy since June 2018: fiscal responsibility, social justice and structural reforms looking to the future. We are faced with budgets that will allow us to reach the cruising speed in the deployment of the recovery plan, with the largest amount of investment in the history -40 billion euros - financed above all with transfers from the European Union, in the fields of science, R & D, sustainable mobility, energy efficiency, education, vocational training, green transition, digital transformation and strengthening the welfare state. They are also budgets that are firmly committed to fiscal responsibility, and I would like to point out today that the European Commission has made public its assessment of Spain's budgetary plan. The European Commission has pointed out that Spain will succeed in reducing its deficit by 2021 and will continue with the positive path in the coming years; it notes that the deficit reduction is aligned with its forecasts, and foresees a strong and sustained recovery of the economy, with an average growth for Spain of 5% in these three years. This confirms, ladies and gentlemen, that thanks to the prudence with which we have acted and the effective measures taken to respond to the pandemic, to protect the productive fabric, employment and incomes of families, we are initiating a strong and sustained recovery that will allow us to gradually absorb the extraordinary fiscal effort made, especially in 2020, and to have a very different exit from the previous financial crisis. These budgets will help to modernise Spain's productive fabric; to promote business growth, productivity, competitiveness; to generate more stable skilled jobs, with better wages, in short, to achieve a sustainable and just recovery that reaches all citizens in all territories. And section 27 of the State General Budgets Bill for 2022 that I come to present today is one of the best exponents of that recovery plan, in particular for the unprecedented investment in digital transformation that will be executed by the ministry that headed it. Firstly, the budget as a whole amounts to more than EUR 5200 million, an increase of 2.3% over 2021. In the two years, 2021 and 2022, the total budget of the ministry will multiply by more than six times, reflecting the substantial change in the implementation of an absolutely key digital agenda for the future of Spain. In addition, in 2022 we will continue to make progress in the deployment of the Recovery Plan. In particular, funding from the plan, from European funds, is increasing by more than 7 %, reaching EUR 4 billion. Finally, the budget provides for increased investment in human capital and the resources required for the effective deployment of this Recovery Plan. By expenditure chapters, staff are increasing by almost 14%, in line with the offer of public employment and the provision of jobs through competition, mainly linked, as I have already pointed out, to the deployment of the Recovery Plan. Current expenditure increases by almost 90% for coin minting, advertising and financial education. Financial expenditure is increased by almost 80% by ICO line expenditure. It should be recalled that, thanks to public guarantees, guarantees of EUR 101 449 million have been deployed to mobilise more than EUR 133 345 million in financing for the entire productive fabric, in particular for SMEs and self-employed people, in 1,131,000 operations. Current transfers, for their part, fall by more than 8%, mainly due to the reduction marked by the European Union in contributions to the debt relief of Greece. In any case, it is important to note that Spain's commitments to multilateral financial institutions are maintained and strengthened, increasing the budget for 2021 by 2022 by more than EUR 26 million with the aim of enhancing Spain's presence in global economic governance and channelling our country's commitment to the 2030 Agenda Sustainable Development Goals, the fight against climate change and the effects of the COVID pandemic. The line for real investment is down almost 43 %, but the line for capital transfers is increasing by almost 30 %. This budgetary reorganization responds to the fact that public sector entities and agencies will be key to the implementation of the Recovery Plan. The chapter on financial operations is down by more than 34%, because, at the moment, we are concentrating action on transfers to the real economy for the deployment of the plan. And within this chapter it is worth noting a budget of 12 million euros to Enisa to support the programme of startups led by women entrepreneurs, which also shows the commitment of the digital agenda to gender equality. If broken down by bodies of the ministry, the undersecretariat responsible for internal management has a budget of 44 million euros, 1.3% more than in the budgets of 2021; the Secretariat of State of Economy and Enterprise Support will have a budget of 806 million euros; the Secretariat of State of Digitization and Artificial Intelligence will have a budget of almost 3 billion euros, which for the most part, more than 2500 million euros, will come from the recovery plan; the Secretariat of State of Telecommunications and Digital Infrastructures will have a budget of more than 1400 million euros, increasing more than 22 % regarding the previous year, and in this case the totality of the funds also comes from the recovery plan. It is worth stopping for a minute to explain in more detail the drive for digitisation represented by these budgets, because the drive for digital transformation is one of the four main axes of the Recovery Plan, an absolutely key lever to relaunch economic growth, reduce inequality and increase productivity and take advantage of all the opportunities offered by new technologies, and all this with a humanist approach, focused on protecting our democratic rights and values. The deployment of this digital agenda is marked by seven aspects, which are perfectly reflected in this draft budget. Firstly, increasing the effort to deploy the connectivity plan. The Single Programme will have almost EUR 750 million in 2022, which is 26% more than in the current year, thus moving towards the goal of having high-speed connectivity at 100 megabits per second and that high-speed connectivity reaches 100 percent of the population by 2025. Digital connectivity is an absolutely key issue in terms of the fight against the demographic challenge, the social and territorial cohesion of our country, the vertebration of our entire territory, the closing of the digital divide between the different age groups, the accompaniment of the most vulnerable population groups or access to public services. It is clear that connectivity is an essential element for such digital transformation. The investment we have planned for 2022 has no precedent. This is more than EUR 13 billion between 2021 and 2022, four times more than the sum of the investment for the years 2019 and 2020. The scope of connectivity and infrastructure was probably the one in which there was most investment in previous years. Secondly, next year we will also increase the effort to boost the 5G, where we will provide almost 650 million euros of investment, 130% more than in 2021, to accelerate the deployment of this technology and get closer to the target of 75% of the population by 2025 and to finance R & D+i in 5G and 6G networks. It should be noted that yesterday the Council of Ministers approved a major transfer of resources to leading research and university centres in the field of R & D & I in advanced 5G and 6G networks. Between 2021 and 2022 we will be devoting almost 900 million euros to the 5G drive, an investment area that was not envisaged in the 2019 and 2020 budgets. Thirdly, we will spend more than EUR 180 million on cybersecurity in 2022. For the period 2021-2022 these investments represent almost 400 million; this is three and a half times more than the 110 million we had in the period 2019-2020. These investments are absolutely fundamental to strengthening the capacities of the public administration, including citizens and our businesses, to tackle the growing cyber threats. Fourthly, we will increase the ministry's investment in digital administration, devoting almost €400 million in 2022, 15% more than in the current year. This leads us, in the period 2021-2022, to an investment of 700 million euros; this is seven times more than the investment budgeted in the period 2019-2020, which will help us to have a more efficient administration, tractor of the whole economy, closer and more accessible to all citizens. Fifthly, we will significantly increase investment in SMEs and self-sustaining digitisation, with a budget of more than EUR 16 billion in 2022; this is approximately 1 billion more than in 2021. This effort will mainly be devoted to the deployment of the Digital Kit. It is a program that we are going to announce precisely tomorrow and that is intended to boost the digital transformation of Spanish SMEs and autonomous by means of a digitalization bonus that allows them to finance a set of services and solutions already available on the market; we are talking about website, presence on the internet, digitization of customer management, provider management, the digital office or data processing. Before the end of the year we will launch this first call for 500 million euros for companies between ten and forty-nine workers and in 2022 we will continue with companies of other sizes. I do not have to highlight the amount of investment, absolutely unprecedented, to support our SME fabric. It is enough to say that the investment during 2021 and 2022 multiplied by 14 the investment made in this field during the period 2019 and 2020, which demonstrates the commitment of the Government of Spain to use the funds of the Recovery Plan to support the activity of our SMEs, the heart of our productive fabric. Sixthly, the budgets of 2022 also provide for EUR 55 million for the development of digital skills, which joins the 980 budgeted in 2021, and thus complements an investment of EUR 1 billion in the period 2021-2022. This will undoubtedly be one of the priorities for the 2022 financial year. It is an investment effort fourteen times greater than that dedicated to digital competitions in the period 2019-2020. This is an absolutely key area for our citizens to be able to take full advantage of the changes and opportunities provided by digital transformation, because we are going to invest not only in the basic digital skills of our citizens, with very special attention to the most vulnerable groups - people at risk of exclusion, our elders, the girls - but also in advanced and specialized digital skills for our workers. This ultimately means investing in economic development and social cohesion. Finally, in 2022, we will continue the deployment of the National Artificial Intelligence Strategy, for which we have provided almost 300 million euros, in addition to the 300 million budgeted for 2021. The investment will focus mainly on the development of data spaces for strategic projects in sectors such as health - this strategic project has already been discussed here a moment ago - and tourism, since, of course, data are essential for the deployment of artificial intelligence. This represents a joint investment of about 600 million euros in the period 2021-2022, more than two and a half times the investment in artificial intelligence for the previous period 2019-2020. Ladies and gentlemen, nobody misses how hard these last twenty-one months have been, following an unprecedented pandemic, and these General State Budgets in this context are now more important than ever. We are facing an absolutely historic moment and the decisions we make today will mark the future of our country. These budgets are good for Spain and for our citizens. They will allow us to achieve a greener, more digital, fairer country for all and all, with full fiscal responsibility. I am convinced that your ladies and gentlemen share the importance of these budgets and will live up to the circumstances. We play a lot and it's time to think about the country with high eyes. The budget of the Ministry of Economy, Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation will be fully devoted to this objective, namely to achieve a strong, sustained, sustainable and inclusive economic recovery, ultimately a fair recovery. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u173	Thank you, President. Very good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. Tomorrow there will be a vote that will take a step further in the adoption of these General State Budgets for the coming year 2022 and I would therefore like to start with a thank you to that parliamentary majority that, from diversity, respect, negotiation and dialogue, is able to build agreements that allow us to strengthen a majority of progressive and plurinational state leadership that gives stability to our country, an indispensable condition in order to be able to face the great challenges and debates that Spain has ahead and, moreover, to do so in a sense of democratic deepening and not of involution and darkness. With humility, because the challenges ahead are giant, these are budgets for the exit of this health emergency with a clear desire for transformation. It is not just about overcoming one of the most painful stages our country has experienced in recent decades, but about getting out of this crisis by facing the crises and structural problems that already existed and that the pandemic has sharpened and made more visible. Of all the lessons learned in these years, the most important, in my opinion, is that which the feminist movement and the popular movements have been pointing out for decades, which is the common thing, the public thing, the fundamental pillar of democracy, which is what sustains us when the country stops completely and they come wrong, but it is also the best indicator, in times of prosperity, of justice and equality in a society. Whatever your postal code, whatever family you are born in or the money you have in your current account, you can have access to the best health care, the best education, all comprehensive care services for women who are victims of male violence. In this regard, the budgets will also allow us to make progress in consolidating a new generation of feminist rights, rights that must effectively reach all women. We are faced with feminist budgets. Investment in public policies for equality continues to have a wide margin of growth, and we are aware of this, but it is no less true that these General State Budgets have a historical departure in feminist public policies: EUR 525 million, a departure that I am now going to detail. 43% of this budget for 2022 will be devoted to equal opportunities programmes; thus, in 2022, weconsolidated the Correspondent Plan, 200 million euros earmarked for the co-responsibility of the State with the tasks of care: cleaning, making food, caring for children or our elderly, tasks which have always been essential for the maintenance of the economy and of life, but which have always remained invisible in the eyes of society and public policies, tasks carried out primarily by women at the expense of the development of their own vital and professional projects. Thus, 200 million for the reconciliation of the working and personal lives of families, especially of mothers and, among them, those who raise alone, those who always assume on their shoulders double and triple days, those who do not have time for themselves, to rest, to protect their health or to go to the cinema or theatre. The Co-responsible Plan must be deployed by the Autonomous Communities throughout the year 2022, and it can use not only the item entered in these budgets but also, as you know, the remaining items for the financial year 2021. Policies such as the Correspondent Plan, together with the impulse of children's schools from 0 to 3 years old, are a seed with still modest investment that we must consolidate, that we must shield to grow, that the State will finally be co-responsible with care, that reconciliation will not be a career of obstacles, that the reconciliation of work, personal and family life will not be a nightmare but a reality for all women. On the other hand, parties to fight against LGBTIphobia, for LGBTI rights and for equal treatment and non-discrimination also grow by more than 130%, because when we say that we are not going to leave anyone behind we are also referring to LGBTI people, racialized people, that they can live without discrimination, without racism, free from violence and in total freedom. They are aware of the commitment of this Government, the Ministry of Equality, to guarantee their rights and the public policies necessary to expand the opportunities for happiness for all people to live a life worthy of being lived. For its part, the Women's Institute will have a specific budget as an autonomous agency of 23.6 million euros, 16% more than in 2021. Since we arrived at the Ministry of Equality, we knew that we had a debt to them and especially to the public workers of the Institute of Women and that as a government we had to return to the institute the weight and entity it deserves after decades by widening equality between women and men, with public officials who have been left behind in this important task. We will therefore reverse with these budgets the path of cuts that this historic institution has suffered so much for all the women of our country. I shall now go on to detail, ladies and gentlemen, the budget allocated by the Ministry of Equality to the eradication of violence against women, an effort for which we allocate more than half of our budget. They will allow me to stop specifically at them, reminding them that tomorrow is the International Day for the Elimination of Violence against Women and that this commemoration also coincides with the 10th anniversary of the Istanbul Convention, which Spain ratified, as you know, in 2014. The fight against sexist violence is a matter of State and cannot be tackled without increasing and solid public funding. For this reason, we allocate EUR 284 million to the prevention, early detection, comprehensive care and reparation of all the violence women suffer as a result of being women. It is a historic budget, ladies and gentlemen, which represents an increase of almost 20% over 2021, thus placing us above what was agreed in the State Pact against Gender Violence. There are 284 million euros, including the funds from the State Pact against Gender Violence for the financing of projects that will enable the autonomous communities and municipalities to deploy, as I said to them, all measures to prevent and raise awareness of early detection, specialized comprehensive care and reparation for women victims of machista violence and to expand them to the institutions of all women, so that they know that institutions are safe places and, above all, that they are not alone. This item also includes the Plan Spain Protects you from Machist Violence, within the framework of the funds for recovery, transformation and resilience. These funds will make it possible to deploy one of the greatest advances that includes the Law on the Comprehensive Guarantee of Sexual Freedom, the Law 'only is yes', as are the crisis centres of full 24-hour care for victims of sexual violence in all the provinces of our country, in addition to the modernization of the 016 Atenpro service and the ones known as bracelets. The fight against macho violence is a priority for this Government. We are demonstrating this with the policies that we have been implementing in these nearly twenty-three months of legislature, with advances such as the Law on the Comprehensive Guarantee of Sexual Freedom, the Children's Law, which, as you know, includes the prohibition of false parental alienation syndrome and the suspension of the regime of visits for abusers in the face of the slightest sign of violence because, as you know, an abuser can never be a good father, the Contingency Plan against all forms of machist violence that we activate during the pandemic, the extension of care services to 016 for all victims of machist violence and the improvement of the working conditions of service workers, the construction of these 24-hour care centres for victims of sexual violence in coordination with the autonomous communities, the updating of protocols and actions against degenerous violence in collaboration with the different institutions and entities of the State Covenant against Gender Violence, Autonomous Communities, the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces and more than one hundred government goes to these feminist entities and organizations, to which they are taking advantage in the actual realization of their work. [[applause]] [[Applauds]] Also initiatives such as the Violet Point, an instrument to involve society as a whole in the fight against macho violence and to let every citizen know how to reach out to a woman who is the victim of violence, not to feel alone and to have access to all the rights that the State has an obligation to guarantee. In the coming months, we will continue to take important steps, including the reparation of a historic debt with thousands of women and feminist movement, as well as the fulfilment of our international human rights commitments. As of 1 January 2022, the Government Delegation against Gender Violence will expand, as you know, detailed and sequenced statistical data on feminicides and all forms of violence against women. Ladies and gentlemen, the greatest political consensus that I believe exists right now in this House is that we will not be able to rest until all violence against women is eradicated, until it is always in time for all women. It is precisely for this reason that I believe it is also of absolute consensus that public policies against sexist violence save lives, as shown by the increase in reports of sexual assault, the increased visibility of such violence, or the fact that this November our country accounts for the lowest number of women murdered at the hands of its partners or ex-partners in the entire historical series. We want to be alive, and to do so we need to continue to make public policies and economic investments to eradicate violence against women, from the absolutest institutional unity and consensus, without fissure. As I said to you, tomorrow we commemorate the International Day for the Eradication of Violence against Women and I would like to conclude by pointing out the opportunity that I believe we have all the political forces in this House - at least all the political forces that in 2017 we make clear that macho violence and the fight against violence against women is a matter of state - to make clear to the whole of society, in a single, uncracked way, that the fight against macho violence remains a matter of state and that no woman is alone, that we are together in this. We have the opportunity to update the opinion of the State Pact against Gender Violence and to do so, as I said, with at least the same consensus as in 2017. We are obliged by women killed, by their sons and daughters, by their families, for whom they are no longer, for whom they continue to fight, for whom they will come to make the Covenant of State permanent and to shield its financing, developing all the progress in public policies and all the legislative changes necessary to be more and more effective as administrations, as institutions, as a State, in the fight against gender-based violence. I would like to thank all the political groups for their willingness and ability to put forward the common goal of combating violence against women, sexist violence, in the face of all the political differences that may distance us, in order to build an agreement that will be one of the most important in this legislature. ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u198	Excuse me, Minister. I ask you, please, a little silence.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u199	Thank you, Madam President. As I was telling you, the Food and Nutrition Security Agency and the Ministry of Consumer Affairs have a line of work to improve eating habits in our country. Why? Because we have a series of indicators of public health, of individual health that are extremely worrying. I give you only one figure: 40% of boys and girls between the ages of six and nine have some kind of overweight or obesity. This is not only a higher probability of adult obesity, and is therefore linked to chronic diseases such as cardiovascular disease, diabetes or hypertension and even some types of cancer, but also has effects at the same time of the life stage of these children in relation to psychological aspects that they may suffer in the social or educational environment. This is a problem that highlights the Aladdin Report of the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, but it is so highlighted by the reports of the Gasol Foundation or the reports of the World Health Organization. Year after year it is seen that we have a problem of eating habits and that this problem is twice as much among the poorest families as among the richest families. This is a problem, therefore, that challenges us about social class, that challenges us about poverty, that challenges us about inequality and that necessarily demands a bold and courageous public policy in the face of complex problems that do not require simple solutions, but complex and multidimensional solutions that have to do with consumption habits, that have to do with areas that are not of consumption - for example, of physical exercise - that have to do with taxation, that have to do with advertising and that has to do with a series of public policies always aimed at improving these indicators that, by the way, have put us at the level of countries such as the United States or Mexico. This is particularly serious in a country like ours, which has a trajectory and history of penetration of the Mediterranean diet and we should be in a much more favourable situation. It is therefore a question of correcting dynamics that are detrimental to the lives and health of the most vulnerable, boys and girls - children's population - and also of working families with fewer resources. And if on top of it is a child or a child of a working family with few resources, the problems are doubly acute. In this sense, we have in the budgets awareness and information campaigns, campaigns that aim to explain to the citizenry what science has accumulated of scientific evidence over the last decades so that they can be taken, freely, with a very wide range, with a very wide repertoire of options, decisions of consumption by parents, mothers and, of course, adult people who want to do so. In addition, this is carried not only by means of campaigns of the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, but also involves the connection with civil society, on the one hand, through the vehicle of consumer associations and, on the other, through agreements with civil society organisations, for example, an agreement is currently under way with the Gasol Foundation, which is also committed to combating child obesity, which is currently in progress and enables parents to explain improvements and tricks so that snacks and breakfasts for young people can have better nutritional components and thus improve their lives. These are therefore public money and resources for better food and therefore for better habits and thus for better health, increased life expectancy and a series of correlations that reach the macro-economic level. Furthermore, you know that we have announced that we hope to have in the shortest possible time a new regulation of advertising aimed at the children's public precisely to bring us to the level of other countries that have already done so, such as Portugal, Norway or even the United Kingdom, following the nutritional profiles of the World Health Organization. It is about establishing new incentive frameworks so that citizens can enjoy better health and do not interrupt or hinder a healthy childhood, which is what our boys and girls deserve. This is the part of food health, but we have to bear in mind that there is growing awareness that global food systems have a clear effect on greenhouse gas emissions. According to FAO, the global food system is already responsible for one third of greenhouse gas emissions. The good news is that there is a broad level of improvement, and the best news is that normally, in general, improving eating habits to have better health also improves the health of the planet itself, because if we go to consumption habits in which we consume locally, we consume closely, we not only protect local employment, but surely, by improving our fruit, vegetable or vegetable components, we will also be improving the ecological impact on the planet. Therefore, in these budgets there is encouragement of campaigns to express support, to encourage, to contribute more tools to local consumption, to consumption in the vicinity, as we did with the Royal Decree on the Flexibility of Livestock Production, to facilitate the producer to provide with the best health guarantees his products directly to the consumer, thus improving the health of the consumer and, at the same time, reducing the emission of greenhouse gases that is associated with this other type of intensive agro-industrial system, whose consequences we are already seeing at the food level, at the ecological level and also at the social level. Secondly, I want to tell you that we are moving on to gambling, gambling houses, gambling halls. I would like to take this opportunity to bring you here an important element. Many years ago, five or six, the social alarm about this matter grows because gambling halls grow, because the facilities in our cities grow, which become spaces for socializing young people who consume their leisure there and, in part, through betting processes and compulsive consumption, with a series of important public health implications for a significant percentage of those participants, who can get to have the extreme case of ludopathy. But before that, there are a number of gradients that affect game disorders. I would like to add that, a month ago, researchers Cañizares and Martínez have published a first academic report on the impact on school performance of the proximity of bookmakers to schools, and the conclusions are devastating. In working class neighborhoods, in popular neighborhoods, in neighborhoods with a lower per capita income where gambling houses or playrooms are located near the schools, performance falls extraordinarily in relation to schools that are located in rich neighborhoods, where betting houses or playrooms and multinationals that manage them have usually decided that it is not the right place for such leisure mechanisms to be established with this danger. This suggests that the policy of gambling, the policy of gambling, the policy carried out by the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, is aimed not only at protecting working families and their users from the pathological and health consequences of such compulsive consumption, but also at other vital dimensions, in this case, the field of education. In addition, the good news is that, as you know, especially if you are a sports fan, since September 1 there is no advertising of gambling houses or games halls on any kind of advertising medium: not on television, not in the print press, not in the digital press, not on the Internet, not on any of the Internet platforms. Since 1 September, the Royal Advertising Decree, which we launched when we arrived at the Government, is in force and which, fortunately, has closed the entry vector of new players who normally, with a wide knowledge of the matter, are incorporated into a sector that, as we said, was very dangerous. I just want to remind you that in the past year 40% of the new players in this field were young between the ages of eighteen and twenty-five, that is to say, the sector had made a great effort to ensure that the most vulnerable people, the elderly but of young age, between the ages of eighteen and twenty-five, were the main depositories of all this. That is why we have campaigns - you will have seen it in the budgets - aimed at information and awareness, because, once the new players' entry vector is closed, we have a large bag of players who can still be unaware of the real risks associated with this type of economic activity, leisure activity, compulsively. That's why we have a real safe play decree, which will be passed in the coming weeks, and at the same time information and awareness campaigns, along with the rate of return of the game to protect working families especially for the class element I talked about earlier and for all players. We have also planned sufficient research and studies to be able to monitor this issue, a matter of growing concern in the European Union. Other countries are imitating our public policies precisely because this is not just a national problem, but an international problem with a considerable extent due to new technologies and the importance of globalisation in the field of sport and sports competitions. We moved on to the third block - in order to be able to comply with this summary within a reasonable time - of budget items, adding something that had happened to me. In the first of the blocks, related to the matter of food security and nutrition, we have a series of items for the improvement of the facilities, of the machinery of the different laboratories that we have in the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, both in Spain and outside of Aesan, that will allow for a much more effective and effective follow-up than what is - undoubtedly fundamental - food security, but also of all the work in general of the Aesan itself and of the Ministry of Consumer Affairs itself. In fact, for the first time, the National Strategy for Food Security and Nutrition, which was planned for 2008 and had so far not had a budget expression, is being budgeted. And when we move to the third block we are talking about the block of consumer protection; what a person might imagine is a Ministry of Consumer Affairs when he has not yet gone into the matter and the question, that is, everything related to the protection of persons who have established a contractual relationship with a large company and who have to make some kind of complaint, request for information or any kind of relationship that would enable him to fulfil his rights, which, as he said, are rights collected at the constitutional level and are rights that should form part of the business culture as elements of competitiveness. In this area, in addition to strengthening all existing instruments, we reinforce one of particular importance, namely the arbitration boards. Arbitral boards are instruments that prevent the judicialization of such conflicts. You know that for business environments where consumer law is not seen as an element of competitiveness - which would be a good decision if at some point it is taken by large companies - but is seen as a kind of ballast, a series of impossible barriers are laid down for many families to be able to exercise rights. We are talking about a labyrinth sometimes administrative so that we can exercise their rights. Well, when the limit is reached, recourse is usually made to the judicial system, which not only collapses the courts, but also slows down the process. Arbitral boards exist at the municipal, provincial and autonomous levels in order to facilitate faster, more effective and non-crash out-of-court decisions. The budgetary reinforcement we are making under this heading is important and the agreements we are concluding with the different entities of the public administration are very remarkable. You also know that in this area we have adopted the Royal Decree of the vulnerable consumer and that we presented a few months ago, the Royal Decree incorporating the sanctioning regime. For the first time, the central Government will be able to supplement the systems and mechanisms of the autonomous communities in order to be able to deal with certain offences or faults by large enterprises and to punish them, so as to discourage further reproduction of that fault. The preliminary draft law on customer service services to improve the efficiency and capacity of consumers when exercising their rights via the telephone is already before the Council of Ministers in the first round. It is a project whose original draft dates from the year 2011 - for a decade as draft - and which now comes forward. I have tried to summarize some of the central issues that appear to be budgeted. They are lines of work of the Ministry of Consumer Affairs, lines of work of the Government of Spain, cross-cutting lines of work that should correspond to certain expanded consensus - it is the naivety that one sometimes has - because we are dealing with sufficiently sensitive issues, such as the protection of vulnerable persons, the protection of children, the improvement of eating habits and the fight against ludopathy. We are talking about matters that will require, under normal conditions, a broad compromise and consensus on the part of the whole House, just as there is already that consensus in the field of society and also civil society. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 32, Ministry of Inclusion, Social Security and Migration, and Section 60, Social Security We move on to the debate in sections 32, Ministry of Inclusion, Social Security and Migration, and 60, Social Security. For the presentation of the sections, the Minister for Inclusion, Social Security and Migration has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u217	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, good afternoon. I compare with you to report on the State General Budgets of the Ministry of Inclusion, Social Security and Migration for 2022, and I am convinced that this is one of the most important appointments this year. The accounts of a Government, its revenue and expenditure programme, are much more than an accounting tool. It is perhaps the best document for setting objectives and setting priorities. In the case of the accounts of the Ministry of Inclusion that I bring to the House today, they clearly reflect our commitment to the existing social protection programmes, but also to the new challenges that we want to face next year. I would like to point out that the Ministry of Inclusion will have a budget of almost EUR 200 billion next year, including contributions from the Recovery Plan, which represents an increase of 6.5% over this year's liquidation forecast, discounting the extraordinary costs incurred by the pandemic. Ladies and gentlemen, this amount will allow us to deploy important policies in all areas of the ministry. I start with the area that is quantitatively most important, that of Social Security and pensions. The budget for 2022 will allow us to effectively implement the reform that is now in parliamentary procedure. It guarantees, once and for all, the purchasing power of all pensioners, but not only will it allow us to guarantee pensions, but it will strengthen the protective action of Social Security in various dimensions. Of the total budget, almost EUR 15 billion will be allocated to contributory pensions, another EUR 2600 to non-contributory pensions, and nearly EUR 18 billion will cover staff pensions, so-called passive classes. In essence, what these numbers do is transmit certainty and security to all our pensioners. For the first time in a decade our pensioners will see how their pensions are revalued thanks to a clear and transparent formula according to the CPI. We thus implement the second recommendation of the Toledo Pact adopted almost unanimously by this House about a year ago. This recommendation required the guarantee by law of the maintenance of the purchasing power of pensioners and, I read in text, presented the annual revaluation of pensions on the basis of the actual CPI as the mechanism to be used to preserve this purchasing power. With these budgets, we also strengthen the solidarity dimension of our pension system by improving, in relative terms, the lowest pensions. Our pension system must preserve the principle of contributoryness, but it must also be able to reduce differences in living conditions and therefore maintain a redistributive dimension. That is why minimum and non-contributory pensions will rise more than inflation, in particular by 3%, because we are convinced that recovery must reach everyone and especially those who have to live on lower incomes. The protection measures during the pandemic have enabled us to overcome the level of employment we had in February 2020 a few months ago, so that even the previous trend of membership is being resumed. This intense job recovery is already clear in the Social Security accounts and will continue to do so next year. In this year, in 2021, we will end up with higher social security contribution income than we had in 2019, and in 2022 we will have 10% more contribution income than then. Just today the European Commission has assessed the government's budget plan for 2022 and, in addition to highlighting the rigour with which these budgets have been drawn up, it has rightly stressed this point, that revenues go extraordinarily well and far beyond the expectations of the European Commission itself, which reflects the intensity of the economic recovery on which we are based. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, and in compliance also with what you approved by an overwhelming majority a year ago in this House, the budgets of 2022 give a new and fundamental step towards the completion of the process of separating social security sources. Pursuant to the first recommendation of the Toledo Pact, the State will contribute 18.4 billion euros to finance the non-contributory policies of Social Security, so-called inappropriate expenses, which will significantly reduce the deficit, and will serve to give a more reliable picture of the state of the accounts in our pension system. I have repeated this many times, in the face of those who regard as cosmetic the separation of sources, for this government the culmination of the transfer of expenses proper to the system that will take place at the end of the full legislature is fundamental to bring certainty to our present and future pensioners. For various reasons for decades, social security budgets have been overburdened with expenditure that does not correspond to them; this is how the Reserve Fund is emptied and the emergence of an accounting deficit in the Social Security accounts, when the contributory system is actually basically in equilibrium, is explained. I would like to thank all the groups who identified this problem and therefore included it in the recommendations of the Toledo Pact. Also in the area of pensions, the Ministry of Inclusion will devote EUR 884 million to combating the gender gap, with a supplement approved by you in the same February of this year of greater scope and much more redistributive power than the maternity supplement to which it replaces. The new supplement, which has received more than 75 000 people on the November payroll - 96% of them are women - corrects the legal problems of the previous mechanism and, more importantly, eliminates its regressive elements and is recognised from the first child, which is the moment when, according to all empirical evidence, the gender gap in employment careers begins to widen, resulting in systematically lower pensions for women. Twenty per cent of the beneficiaries in this month of November, and nearly 15 000 women, today receive this benefit in the form of an additional pension because they had only one child, a situation in which they were not covered in the previous model. This government, ladies and gentlemen, also wants to promote co-responsibility in raising children. That is why we dedicated 29 billion euros in 2022 to the child birth and child care allowance. Like this year, both parents will have sixteen weeks of benefit each, and will be able to split their permits to adapt them to the needs of each family. This policy puts us at the forefront of Europe in equality, reconciliation and co-responsibility policies. It is a central measure to protect children in their first months of life, their mothers and parents, and to help bridge the unacceptable gender gap created by the unequal sharing of family responsibilities between men and women. Secondly, the 2022 budget also includes more than EUR 3 billion for minimum living income and inclusion policies. This will be strengthened as the most appropriate central tool to combat child poverty through the implementation of the child support complement, increasing the redistributive power of the mechanism. The minimum living income, already reaching around 400,000 households, with almost 900,000 people, will continue to be deployed throughout the national territory, with a 3% increase in its amounts, the same increase as the minimum and non-contributory pensions. Our goal is to reach the greatest number of vulnerable households and, to that end, we are putting in place new measures to reach them. Within a few days, the third sector of social action will join the municipal social services in accrediting special situations of vulnerable people, and will help to reach households where the minimum living income has not been reached so far. We will also continue to deploy innovative tools to incorporate other potential beneficiaries who have not yet applied for minimum living income. In addition, minimum living income, which has always had the fight against child poverty as one of its main objectives, will reinforce its impact among children with the new complement of child support. This supplement involves the establishment of additional support per child with thresholds higher than those of the minimum living income, i.e. they will be able to access it not only those who already receive or qualify for the IMV, but also low-income households, but which exceed to a certain level the levels of access of the minimum living income. All these households will receive additional support for each child, consisting of 100 euros per month for each child between 0 and 3 years, 70 euros for each child between 3 and 6 years and 50 euros for each child between 6 and 18 years. Ladies and gentlemen, minimum living income is also an innovative, cutting-edge benefit. To improve our knowledge of the most effective inclusion strategies, various conventions, some already signed, will be deployed in 2022 to launch and evaluate different inclusion routes. Together with autonomous administrations, local authorities and entities in the third sector of social action, we are already developing some pilot projects which, in the field of education, digitisation or access to housing, will allow us to know what types of interventions work best in order to be able to scale them up to more beneficiaries in the future. Families with children, with special attention to single-parent families, long-term unemployed and poor workers, the most disadvantaged groups, ultimately the most vulnerable, are a priority in the commitment to the inclusion of this ministry. Finally, but not least, our ministry's budget has an important boost in migration. We will strengthen our capacity for international reception and protection, and we will put in place a new system of social consultation that allows us to manage resources more easily, efficiently and flexibly. Good management of migration, ladies and gentlemen, is an absolute priority for this Government. This was demonstrated when we had to put our reception system in place in record time to facilitate the evacuation of families from Afghanistan last summer. The system continues to work day by day for our collaborators and their families from Afghanistan to rebuild their lives among us. The migration area will have 634 million euros in the 2022 budget, 17% more than the one to be implemented this year. This amount will enable us to strengthen the capacity of our reception system through new centres of our own, solving a deficit that made management especially rigid and complicated. We will dedicate EUR 55 million next year to the development of new resources throughout the territory, which will allow us a more agile management and more adapted to the new migration reality. It is also our commitment that these investments contribute to social and economic dynamism in areas affected by depopulation. The budgets of 2022 will also allow us to make another important improvement in our management, by being able to move from the current framework based on grants to another centered on the social concert. This will allow us to have a closer and more stable link with third-sector partners, to be able to develop medium- and long-term strategies and ultimately to improve the governance of the system. In addition, in 2022, the Ministry of Inclusion, Social Security and Migration will redouble its commitment to areas of the country under increased migratory pressure. Ladies and gentlemen, these 2022 budgets will be fundamental to some of the reforms we have been working on for many months, as we committed ourselves at the beginning of the parliamentary term. These budgets will enable us to implement the modernisation of the pension system under way in Parliament. This will ensure the purchasing power of our pensioners forever and strengthen the sustainability of the system with a more modern and equitable model. In addition, it will strengthen minimum vital income as the most powerful tool for combating poverty, with a special focus on children, through the deployment of inclusion routes. Finally, in the field of migration, we will be able to strengthen the system of reception and international protection, with the implementation of new places of our own and with better management when implementing the new model based on the social concert. Ultimately, these budgets will enable us to improve the lives of pensioners, workers and their families, vulnerable households and migrants. These are budgets designed to make our country more fair, to make our citizens better protected and, ultimately, to bring recovery and progress to all citizens. Budgets set these priorities because it is the society to which we aspire. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-24-CD211124.u245	Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. Good afternoon, President. Thank you. The budget of the Ministry of Universities corresponds to section 33 of the State General Budgets. The total consolidated budget is €462.89 million. I would like to highlight something important, that only 4% of the budget is for staff and goods and services, that is, 96% is for the university community and their families. From 2020 to 2022 the budget of the Ministry of Universities has increased by 77 %, if we consider European programmes, and without these programmes by 15 %. What do we propose to spend these funds on the 2022 budgets? First, in university teachings for which the ministry is responsible, not what is taught in every public university that depends on the autonomous communities. University education represents 138.7 million, including a substantial increase in the budget for the National University of Distance Education, a university that, as you know, is fundamental in our system and increasingly. For example, it is a university that next week, in December, will start organizing the national nursing test, with 35,000 women candidates to fill the nursing positions that the Government is creating. That could not have been done without the National University of Distance Education. Its budget now stands at 91.3 million. This chapter also includes the co-financing of the European University Programme, through which our universities are interlinked and closely allied with European universities in a number of countries. Secondly, aid to students other than scholarships - which I will see later - in particular the co-financing of the Erasmus Plus programme, which is increasing by 33%, to EUR 40 million. Thirdly, research expenditure, programme 463.A, increased by 9,2 %, which amounts to EUR 114 million. This includes training grants for university professors, which reach 80.8 million, and also Beatriz Galindo grants for attracting talent to Spanish universities, which are increased by 50%. Fourthly, we are going to spend it on recharging and digitizing the Spanish university system. This is done from European funds managed by the Ministry of Universities. The total amount of this European Union financing instrument is EUR 480 million. In the year 2021 will be executed 203.9 million euros, in the year 2022 will be executed 147 million euros and in the year 2023 will be executed 129 million euros. As you can see, we have advanced as far as possible the allocation of these funds to the ministry's budget in order to promote their immediate absorption and making available to universities in order to achieve the fastest possible recovery of these funds. The ministry has already committed almost 100% of the appropriations for these European programmes, not just for the 2021 financial year, but for the entire 2021-2023 period. With regard to European appropriations for 2022, they are incorporated into the budget of the Ministry of Universities through two programmes. The first is the Programme for the training and training of teaching and research staff, with EUR 125 million, to implement the second annuality of aid to public universities, for the call for aid for the retraining of university staff, through three modalities: firstly, the modalities of the aid we call Margarita Salas, to allow young doctors to stay multiannual research in centres of international and national prestige; secondly, the aid we call Maria Zambrano, for the attraction of talent; and thirdly, additional funds for the qualification of university teachers through training outside their own university. As early as April 2021 my ministry has processed the royal decree regulating the direct granting of grants to public universities for the redevelopment of the Spanish university system. Through this mechanism and its grant order, all the credits have already been transferred to the Spanish public universities for the call for aid for a total of EUR 125 million. The grant of the second annuality of EUR 125 million will be processed in 2022. The objective set through these European programmes and committed to Europe is to grant at least 2600 recasting aid up to the end of 2023. I would like to tell you that there has never been such a massive and extensive programme of recasting, focusing above all on recasting in national and international centres, that between one and three years allows our young doctors and university staff as a whole to acquire the highest international excellence. The second programme linked to European funds managed by the Ministry of Universities is the Digital Infrastructure Improvement Programme, equipment, technologies, teaching and evaluation of our universities. In the proposal for the year 2022 it is endowed with 22 million euros. This amount has been transferred to public universities as a second annuality, not all, is the second annuality of the Modernization and Digitalization Plan of the University System, with the aim of increasing its digitalization rate by at least 10% compared to 2019. In this year 2021 the Ministry of Universities has issued another royal decree - last July - regulating the direct granting of grants to Spanish public universities for the modernization and digitization of the university system. Its award order has also brought these grants to universities for an amount of EUR 65.7 million. This programme is co-financed with European funds, together with the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Digital Transformation, for an additional 50 million in several years. Fifthly, we maintain in the budget the same funding as in 2021 for cooperation and educational promotion abroad. In addition, I would like to point out that the Ministry of Universities, as your ladies and gentlemen know, has three autonomous bodies essential to the university system, such as the National Agency for the Evaluation of Quality and Accreditation, Aneca, the Menéndez Pelayo International University and the Spanish Service for the Internationalization of Education. The budgets of the three agencies are increasing. [[The lady]] Beyond the budget of the Ministry of Universities, one important issue should be noted within the framework of the Government as a whole. As you know, our ministry manages, together with the Ministry of Education and Vocational Training, the state scholarship system. Following the comprehensive reform carried out jointly by the two ministries in 2020, applying the programmatic principle that the grant is a subjective duty, the State General Budgets allocate 2,022,219 million to scholarships, i.e. 109 million more than in 2021, the highest amount in history, with a 40% increase over 2020. In the case of university students this has meant that fellow students will double with full scholarship from 2019-2020 to 2020-2021, reaching a total of fellows with total or partial scholarship of 362 783. This is essential, because we know, according to the studies available, that the economic difficulties resulting from the absence of a scholarship are the main cause of the high drop-out from our system, which at the same time is a human waste, above all, and an economic waste. I would also like to point out that 73% of the investments that are programmed in the general budgets for scientific and technical equipment benefit universities because, as you know, they are the main producers of knowledge in our country. I would also like to point out something important concerning the strengthening and rejuvenation of teaching and research staff. The draft Law on General Budgets of the State contains not only numerical statements, but also important provisions that make up its structure. [[The Lord]] I would like to underline one aspect that is essential from a concrete point of view, within our objective of strengthening the teaching and research capacity of universities; namely, it establishes the consideration of teaching and research staff and administrative and service staff as a priority sector. This has a concrete consequence, that the planned replacement rate will pass in the budgets from 2022 to 120 %. In other words, 120 people will be integrated into the university system for every 100 retirements. Thus, according to our forecasts, in the next decade they retired between 30 and 50% - a minimum and a maximum - of teachers and permanent researchers. In that regard, these places should therefore be replenished at a rate of 120. In addition, the same provision in the budgets also allows the accumulation of the replacement rate of one or more sectors or groups, priority or not, to other priority sectors or groups such as universities, the education system in general. In particular - look how important this is - public administrations will be able to assign to universities of their competence and public universities will be able to assign among them fees, quotas of these fees, naturally with the authorization of the public administrations on which they depend. These measures demonstrate our strong commitment in the ministry and in the Government as a whole to rejuvenating teaching and research staff, as well as administrative and service staff, thereby reversing a situation that threatened the sustainability of the system in the coming years. In summary, ladies and gentlemen, the General State Budgets and the budget of the Ministry of Universities within this framework significantly increase public spending and investment in fulfilment of the Government's objectives of a transformation of our educational system which results in a substantial increase in productivity, the economy and, at the same time, a fair recovery that benefits all citizens. Thank you so much for your attention. [[Applauds]]
